# Cassandra Test Index — SSTables, Data Correctness & Data-Loss Prevention
> Generated by a fleet of 403 subagents (one per test file), each reading the full source of an Apache Cassandra 5.0 test and characterizing it. Source tree: `~/projects/cassandra`. Later augmented (issue #1408) with 4 db/cql3 serialization tests referenced by the parity manifest.
**Scope:** 407 test files matching SSTable / compaction / commitlog / repair / compression / serialization / corruption / scrub / verify / checksum / bloom / memtable-flush / tombstone / digest. Approx **3,604** `@Test` methods.
## How to read this
- **Relevance** ranks each test's bearing on *SSTable data correctness & data-loss prevention*:
  - 🔴 **High** — directly exercises on-disk format/serialization, compaction merge correctness, tombstone/TTL purging, corruption detection/scrub/verify, checksums, or commitlog recovery.
  - 🟡 **Med** — touches these indirectly (repair coordination, streaming, tooling).
  - ⚪ **Low** — mostly orchestration, config, or metrics.
- **CQLite relevance** notes whether the behavior matters for a Rust SSTable *reader/compactor*.

## Detailed index by category

<a id="sstable-format"></a>
### SSTable On-Disk Format  (11 files)

#### 🔴 High · `SSTableIdGenerationTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/distributed/org/apache/cassandra/distributed/test/SSTableIdGenerationTest.java`
- **Subsystem:** `org.apache.cassandra.io.sstable` · **~@Test methods:** 6
- **Purpose:** Validates SSTable descriptor naming and handling when mixing sequential IDs and UUID-based IDs across node restarts, compaction strategies, streaming, and backup/restore operations. Ensures data correctness and no data loss during transitions between ID schemes.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Sequential SSTables created before UUID enablement are readable and counted correctly
  - UUID SSTables created after enabling UUID can coexist with legacy sequential SSTables
  - Node startup fails with clear error when UUID disabled but UUID SSTables present
  - Mixed sequential/UUID SSTables compact correctly with Size-Tiered, Time-Window, and Leveled strategies, preserving row counts
  - Streaming replicates mixed SSTable ID types correctly to target nodes with UUID enabled and disabled
  - Snapshots preserve both sequential and UUID SSTable references and can be restored
  - Incremental backups capture both ID types and restore correctly via bulk load
  - SSTable activity tracking (system.sstable_activity) handles both ID types separately in both legacy and v2 tables
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent data loss during compaction of mixed-scheme SSTables by verifying row counts match expected totals (9 rows from overlapping partitions across 4 SSTables). Ensures streaming and restore operations preserve all data when transitioning between ID schemes. Guards against data duplication or loss when merging SSTables with different identifier types.
- **CQLite relevance:** CQLite's compaction engine must parse and merge SSTables with both SequenceBasedSSTableId and UUIDBasedSSTableId, correctly identifying descriptors and preserving data during merge. The test validates that compaction merge semantics work across ID types - critical for CQLite's ability to read and re-compact mixed Cassandra 5.0 clusters that have transitioned between ID schemes.

#### 🔴 High · `SSTableReverseIteratorTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/unit/org/apache/cassandra/db/columniterator/SSTableReverseIteratorTest.java`
- **Subsystem:** `org.apache.cassandra.db.columniterator` · **~@Test methods:** 1
- **Purpose:** Validates that SSTableReverseIterator correctly traverses indexed partition blocks in reverse order and gracefully handles empty blocks resulting from dropped columns, ensuring reverse-order queries return correct data.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Reverse iteration over indexed partitions with multiple blocks
  - Skips empty blocks resulting from dropped column filtering
  - Correctly handles ORDER BY DESC queries after schema evolution
  - Preserves data correctness when some index blocks contain no unfiltereds after column removal
  - Works with BigTable index entries containing blockCount > 2
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent data loss or iterator crashes when reverse scanning partitions with empty blocks. Without this fix, queries would either fail or skip valid data rows that come after empty blocks in reverse order.
- **CQLite relevance:** CQLite must correctly implement reverse-order traversal of Cassandra's BigTable index blocks when reading Data.db/Index.db. Empty blocks from dropped columns are a real SSTable format scenario; incorrect handling would cause queries with ORDER BY DESC to return wrong results or fail during compaction/merge of evolved schemas.

#### 🔴 High · `CQLSSTableWriterTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/unit/org/apache/cassandra/io/sstable/CQLSSTableWriterTest.java`
- **Subsystem:** `org.apache.cassandra.io.sstable` · **~@Test methods:** 37
- **Purpose:** End-to-end testing of SSTable generation via CQLSSTableWriter across multiple SSTable formats (BigFormat, BTI) and features, validating that generated files are correctly encoded and readable by the query engine after on-disk deserialization.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Round-trips data across multiple SSTable formats (BigFormat, BTI); validates format markers and post-load query correctness
  - Verifies buffer-size-triggered SSTable rotation respects partition boundaries and memory limits
  - Tests deletion encoding (partition delete, range delete, cell delete, value delete) with tombstone serialization and timestamp ordering
  - Validates timestamp-based MVCC conflict resolution and TTL expiration with local deletion time recording
  - Tests frozen UDT, nested UDT, and vector type serialization with full codec round-trips
  - Validates compression handling including ZstdDictionary with dictionary-aware compressor
  - Ensures concurrent writers produce valid independent SSTable files without cross-thread corruption
  - Tests SAI index building and skipping with index descriptor completion validation
  - Validates constraint violation detection at write time
  - Tests sorted writer with configurable size limits producing correctly-distributed multi-SSTable output
  - Confirms SSTableProducedListener callbacks align with actual on-disk files

TOC.txt is critical metadata that lists all component files present in an SSTable generation, enabling safe file discovery and integrity tracking across flushes, compactions, and streaming.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Appends component references in lexicographically sorted order (Data.db, Filter.db, Statistics.db, TOC.txt) to ensure deterministic manifests
  - Ensures idempotent TOC updates—calling updateTOC twice produces no duplication or ordering inconsistencies
  - Validates that missing write target directories raise FSWriteError, preventing silent TOC creation failures
  - Performs auto-discovery of orphaned components (e.g. Filter.db) when TOC is missing and reconstructs it from disk scan
  - Loads TOC with skipMissing=false to preserve record of components listed in manifest even if files missing on disk
  - Loads TOC with skipMissing=true to filter out missing files and return only physically present components
  - Rewrites existing TOC files atomically, replacing stale manifest entries and re-sorting lexicographically
  - Handles edge case where no components exist on disk—empty component set, no TOC file created
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents data loss by ensuring TOC.txt accurately reflects which component files belong to an SSTable generation. Mismatched or corrupted TOC can cause: (1) silent loss of components during compaction if manifest doesn't list required files, (2) orphaned bloom filters or statistics ignored during repair/streaming, (3) unsafe recovery logic that relies on TOC to identify files to preserve/delete, (4) duplicate or missing components written during flush if idempotency fails. Orphaned file discovery prevents unintended data retention.
- **CQLite relevance:** High relevance to CQLite SSTable readers. CQLite must accurately discover and validate all component files in an SSTable generation. If reading a legacy SSTable without TOC, the auto-discovery logic (testLoadOrCreateWithDiscoveredComponents) is essential—CQLite mirrors this by scanning directories to find Data.db, Index.db, Filter.db, Statistics.db, etc. The sortedness guarantee ensures deterministic file enumeration. The skipMissing behavior informs how CQLite should handle partially-written SSTables during streaming or recovery—whether to trust TOC metadata for missing files or only read physically present components. Compaction/merging logic depends on accurate component manifests to avoid losing statistics or filter metadata.

  - Column altered then dropped: typeMap retained to handle Int32 on-disk vs Long in drop record
  - Static column type changed: detected via same mismatch logic as regular columns
  - Collection multi-cell flag changed (frozen vs multi-cell): type equality detects mismatch, typeMap retained
  - Column kind changed after write (static to regular): SSTable opened with mismatched schema uses on-disk type info to correctly interpret rows
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent data corruption during deserialization. If SerializationHeader fails to track type mismatches, altered columns deserialize with wrong codec (incorrect values or buffer overruns), dropped columns skip wrong byte counts, and collection multi-cell vs frozen incompatibilities return garbage.
- **CQLite relevance:** HIGH. CQLite's compaction/merge must correctly deserialize rows from SSTables written with older schemas. SerializationHeader typeMap determines which codec deserializes each column. If CQLite ignores this metadata, merged output silently corrupts data. CQLite must: (1) extract typeMap during SSTable open, (2) use recorded on-disk types (not current schema) during row deserialization, (3) propagate correct types through compaction output.

- **Scenarios:**

  - Serializes and round-trips diverse CQL types (primitives, UDTs, collections, vectors, frozen maps/sets)
  - Enforces timestamp/TTL semantics via USING TIMESTAMP and USING TTL write options
  - Respects buffer and partition boundaries (flushes multi-partition rows when buffer overflows)
  - Handles deletions (DELETE, DELETE RANGE, DELETE VALUE) with proper tombstone encoding
  - Validates data read from loaded SSTables matches written values (full parity checks)
  - Tests concurrent writer threads producing independent SSTables without corruption
  - Supports secondary indexes (SAI) and Zstd compression dictionaries
  - Rejects invalid operations (counter updates, unset clustering keys, constraint violations)
  - Tests date/time type serialization with both old and new format representations
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent data loss and corruption by validating: (1) round-trip fidelity - all written cells survive deserialization; (2) tombstone integrity - deletions are correctly encoded and respected during future compaction; (3) TTL enforcement - expired rows are purged as expected; (4) timestamp ordering - newer writes override stale ones; (5) multi-partition integrity - large partitions do not corrupt adjacent rows when buffer limits are exceeded.
- **CQLite relevance:** CQLite reads Cassandra 5.0 SSTables (Data.db, Index.db, Statistics.db). These tests validate on-disk format correctness at write time. CQLite parsers must handle: (1) Data.db/Index.db serialization format (flags, cell encoding, tombstone markers); (2) compression blocks and checksums; (3) tombstone and TTL cells (CQLite's merge/compaction must recognize deletion semantics); (4) multi-generation SSTables (unsorted vs sorted writer output). Test parity checks ensure CQLite reads will match Cassandra's own deserialization.

### Compression & Compression Dictionaries  (31 files)

#### 🔴 High · `CompressionUpgradeTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/distributed/org/apache/cassandra/distributed/upgrade/CompressionUpgradeTest.java`
- **Subsystem:** `org.apache.cassandra.io.compress` · **~@Test methods:** 1
- **Purpose:** Validates that compressed SSTables created with different codecs (Zstd with min/default/max levels, Snappy, Deflate, LZ4) remain readable and queryable across Cassandra version upgrades, ensuring cross-version compression format stability.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Reads Zstd-compressed SSTables at extreme compression levels (-131072, 22) across version upgrades
  - Verifies Snappy, Deflate, and LZ4 compression remain readable post-upgrade
  - Validates INSERT/SELECT operations on compressed tables in both old and new version nodes
  - Confirms data integrity (row content 1,1,1) survives decompression in upgraded nodes
  - Tests mixed-node clusters where old-version nodes coexist with upgraded nodes reading same compressed data
  - Ensures CompressionMetadata and block-level decompression work identically across versions
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent data corruption from compression codec incompatibilities across upgrades. A failure would indicate compressed SSTables become unreadable post-upgrade, causing silent data loss if decompression silently fails or returns corrupted rows.
- **CQLite relevance:** CRITICAL for CQLite SSTable readers. CQLite must correctly parse and decompress all four codecs (Zstd, Snappy, Deflate, LZ4) in real-world production SSTables. This test validates that codec parameters (especially Zstd compression_level range) are correctly preserved in CompressionMetadata, which CQLite's decompression layer must respect. Failure to read any codec would prevent valid SSTables from being processed.

#### 🔴 High · `ZstdCompressionDictionaryTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/unit/org/apache/cassandra/db/compression/ZstdCompressionDictionaryTest.java`
- **Subsystem:** `org.apache.cassandra.db.compression` · **~@Test methods:** 13
- **Purpose:** Validates Zstd compression dictionary lifecycle, reference counting, serialization, and checksum integrity. Ensures dictionary objects are safely managed across concurrent decompression operations without use-after-free bugs or data corruption.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Equality and hash code behavior for dictionary identity
  - Dictionary caching per compression level (1, 3, 6)
  - Decompression dictionary caching and reuse
  - Invalid compression level boundary checking
  - Reference counting lifecycle (ref/tryRef/release)
  - Access protection after dictionary release
  - Concurrent access from 100 parallel tasks with different compression levels
  - Binary serialization round-trip with metadata preservation
  - Deserialization caching behavior for duplicate payloads
  - Checksum validation detects corrupted dictionary data during deserialization
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent decompression failures and data corruption by enforcing checksum validation during dictionary deserialization, reference-counted access to prevent use-after-free bugs, and safe concurrent access patterns. Corrupted dictionary checksums would cause decompression of SSTable blocks to produce garbage silently.
- **CQLite relevance:** Critical for CQLite's compression support. CQLite reads Cassandra 5.0 SSTables with Zstd compression dictionaries embedded in metadata. This test validates: (1) correct round-trip serialization format so CQLite parsers can extract and deserialize dictionaries, (2) checksum validation ensures corrupted dictionaries are rejected before attempting decompression, (3) concurrent dictionary access patterns inform thread-safe decompression pipeline design when multiple readers access the same SSTable.

This test validates checksum validation, chunk boundary handling, and corruption detection that a reader depends on. The overflow test (CASSANDRA-15595) is critical: if chunk indexing overflows, a reader seeking to a position could silently read from the wrong block, causing data loss.

CQLite's decompression parser must match these semantics exactly—if a Cassandra-written SSTable uses BadCompressor (or real compressors), CQLite must successfully round-trip the data. Compaction correctness here ensures CQLite receives well-formed compressed blocks.

- **CQLite relevance:** Critical for CQLite's SSTable reader: (1) Zstd decompression relies on dictionary availability; premature release causes silent data corruption on read. (2) When CQLite merges/compacts multiple SSTables with Zstd+dictionary compression, it must not free dictionaries until all metadata instances close (multi-SSTable scenario in testMultipleMetadataInstancesSharingDictionary). (4) Compression codec (ICompressor) must remain valid across dictionary cache lifecycle for correct decompression during streaming/compaction.

  - Reads through uncompressed path when compression overhead exceeds savings (maxCompressedLength = 0)
  - Prevents integer overflow when reading from file positions >4GB (tests 4GB–8GB boundaries)
  - Verifies data integrity: decompressed output byte-for-byte matches original uncompressed input
  - Property-based testing with random compression parameters, writer options, and file sizes (up to 64KB per test)
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent data corruption from (1) decompression errors going undetected, (2) truncated SSTable chunks being read as partial/wrong data, (3) integer overflow silently skipping/misreading chunks at large offsets (>4GB), and (4) incorrect data being returned when SSTable files are corrupted but still accessible.
- **CQLite relevance:** CRITICAL: CQLite reads compressed Data.db chunks using the exact decompression logic tested here (LZ4, Snappy, Deflate, Zstd). The tests validate (1) correctness of chunk decompression and offset calculation (CQLite's compaction/merge must decompress all source chunks accurately), (2) integer-overflow safety for 64-bit file offsets (CQLite merges multi-GB SSTables), and (3) corruption detection (CQLite must fail safely on truncated Data.db rather than returning partial rows). Edge-case handling (partial chunks, uncompressed fallback) directly maps to CQLite's streaming reader logic.

Ensures CompressionInfo.db stores the concrete (portable) compressor class name via serializedAs() so peers and future restarts can deserialize chunks regardless of plugin availability. - **CQLite relevance:** HIGH - CQLite reads SSTable CompressionInfo.db files to decompress Data.db chunks. This test ensures on-disk metadata records the concrete compressor class name (e.g., SnappyCompressor, LZ4Compressor), not wrapper names. Correct serializedAs() behavior is critical for CQLite's decompression path to work across different Cassandra deployments and plugin configurations.

- **CQLite relevance:** format parsing — CQLite reads checksummed Index.db and Statistics.db blocks in SSTable 5.0. Validating checksum computation ensures CQLite's checksum verification logic correctly detects corruption in on-disk data. If checksums fail in Cassandra's writer, CQLite will reject the same data with matching logic.

  - Deletion markers in different SSTables require checking prior SSTables to confirm rows are truly absent
  - Row deletion vs cell deletion on compact vs non-compact tables affects liveness propagation across SSTables
  - Column index skipping with range filters on large partitions minimizes unnecessary SSTable reads
  - Static columns and partition tombstones with range deletes interact correctly with merge strategy
  - Schema evolution (ALTER TABLE DROP COMPACT STORAGE) forces additional SSTable reads due to lost schema hints
  - Overlapping SSTable ranges with mixed timestamps test lower/upper bound optimization correctness
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent data loss from faulty SSTable merge logic: (1) deletion markers in newer SSTables must not suppress live cells in older SSTables without timestamp verification; (2) early read termination must not occur when unseen SSTables contain relevant data; (3) index skipping must not omit SSTables that fall outside a slice predicate but contain deletion markers affecting the result
- **CQLite relevance:** Critical for CQLite compaction/merge implementation. CQLite's compactor must: (1) merge SSTables in timestamp order to produce correct tombstone visibility; (2) respect partition plus clustering key ordering when deciding SSTable access; (3) handle deletion markers correctly across SSTable boundaries; (4) recognize when indexed column ranges allow skipping SSTables that dont overlap the predicate

  - Segregates partition tokens into correct SSTables based on range membership
  - Maintains reference counts and lifecycle state invariants across anticompaction
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent data loss and incorrect reads during repair by (1) ensuring no partition data is lost when SSTables are split by range; (2) correctly marking which data is in-repair (pending-repair) vs unrepaired, so repair does not double-apply or skip changes; (3) validating range preconditions so out-of-bounds ranges fail fast rather than silently dropping data; (4) ensuring disk space accounting remains correct after splits (no loss of granularity); (5) catching malformed repair sessions before they corrupt SSTable state.
- **CQLite relevance:** CQLite's read-only compaction/merge semantics depend on anticompaction invariants: when merging SSTables from different repair stages (pending vs unrepaired), CQLite must respect the partition-key boundary splits that anticompaction creates. CQLite does not implement anticompaction itself (write-only), but understanding these splits is critical for correct merging of heterogeneous SSTables in repair-aware contexts. Token range containment logic informs bloom-filter and index range queries during reads.

- **CQLite relevance:** For CQLite's compaction merge: Bucket/level algorithms inform SSTable read prioritization and merge sequencing across generations. Expiration semantics (TTL purging via max-local-deletion-time, gc_grace_seconds filtering) must be replicated during compaction to avoid stale data in merged output. Parallel shard execution requires non-overlapping token ranges to be preserved—relevant to CQLite's multi-threaded merge algorithm. Understanding which SSTables are selected (not just that compaction happens) is critical for incremental query plans and partial-table scans.

By enforcing early termination on disk-space failures, the test guards against cascading failures in multi-level compaction pipelines.
- **CQLite relevance:** High for compaction implementations. CQLite's compaction logic must read multiple input SSTables and produce merged output. This test validates the prerequisite disk-space check that compactors must perform before initiating I/O. If a reader/compactor ignores disk availability, it risks partial SSTable writes that corrupt the index — the disk-space validation shown here is a critical input precondition.

  - Instruments BootstrapAndJoin.executeNext() with ByteBuddy to intercept all join sequence state transitions

However, bootstrap-time compaction suspension could produce malformed SSTables (out-of-order tombstones, unpurged deletes, unmerged logical timestamps) that violate ordering invariants assumed by CQLite's merge-compaction engine during later operations.

#### 🟡 Med · `CancelCompactionsTest.java`

Tests that lifecycle transactions abort cleanly (scanners, controllers, transactions closed) so incomplete merges do not leave overlapping or partially-written SSTables.
- **CQLite relevance:** Informs compaction merge correctness for CQLite's compactor. When merging multiple SSTables, cancellation must be atomic (not mid-row). CQLite's merge logic must handle aborted transactions safely without leaving partial writes. However, this test does not validate actual merge semantics, just cancellation coordination.

  - Tracks and enforces repaired/pending-repair/unrepaired/transient repair state markers during assignment

Classification tests ensure mutually exclusive holder assignment prevents double-compaction or missed compaction.

Understanding repair state boundaries informs merge key range validation. Disk boundary logic may inform range-aware SSTable selection. However, CQLite currently does not implement concurrent upgrade tracking or time-window compaction strategies (TWCS).

However, CQLite's primary focus is reading existing SSTables, not live compaction orchestration. The hotness/read-rate weighting is Cassandra-specific cluster behavior; a local reader may not need this. Useful for compaction testing and validation, but not critical for basic read correctness.

#### 🟡 Med · `ControllerTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/unit/org/apache/cassandra/db/compaction/unified/ControllerTest.java`
- **Subsystem:** `org.apache.cassandra.db.compaction.unified` · **~@Test methods:** 23
- **Purpose:** Tests the Unified Compaction Strategy Controller configuration, validation, and sharding logic that determines how SSTables are grouped and compacted based on target size, growth modifiers, and shard count parameters.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Validates configuration options (target_sstable_size, min_sstable_size, scaling parameters) with bounds checking and overflow detection
  - Parses and normalizes scaling parameters (e.g., T4, L2, N notation) and detects malformed expressions
  - Calculates shard count based on SSTable size with configurable growth modifiers (0.0, 0.5, 1.0, 0.333)
  - Enforces min/max shard count constraints respecting base_shard_count and MAX_SHARD_SPLIT limits
  - Handles edge cases: NaN sizes, zero sizes, Long.MAX_VALUE overflow, sizes below minimum thresholds
  - Detects CASSANDRA-20398 regression (12E899 B exceeding Long.MAX_VALUE)
  - Truncation prevention: verifies Integer arithmetic (target*0.7) computed as Long to avoid Integer.MAX_VALUE bounds
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents data loss by validating compaction strategy configuration before applying it to SSTable sets. Incorrect sharding/grouping could lead to: (1) missing compaction opportunities causing unbounded LSM tree growth, (2) incomplete merges leaving tombstones unprocessed, (3) miscalculation of survival ratios causing premature expiration of valid SSTables if aggressive expiration is enabled with overlooked overlaps.
- **CQLite relevance:** Moderate relevance to CQLite compaction/merge logic. CQLite's on-disk compaction writer must respect the same sharding strategy when producing merged SSTables. Configuration validation prevents invalid controller states that could cause malformed output SSTables. However, CQLite's primary concern is reading existing SSTables; this tests orchestration logic rather than format encoding/decoding. If CQLite implements write-side compaction merging (M5.2), it must replicate this controller's parameter validation to ensure output correctness.

  - Concurrent queries during SSTable upgrade/compaction return correct row counts and values

  - Dropping indexes mid-compaction cleanly removes all index files and disk usage
  - Segment builder pooling doesn't accumulate during sharded compaction (memory bounded)

Detects failures during early-open checkpoints and SSTable writer commits that could leave indexes in inconsistent state or orphan index components on disk, leading to stale data in subsequent queries. Validates that concurrent index operations (build, drop) don't lose table or index data.
- **CQLite relevance:** Low — CQLite reads finalized SSTable Data.db/Index.db files; this test covers SAI index-specific coordination during compaction, not the SSTable format itself. Relevant only if CQLite needs to handle SSTables that contain SAI index components (which it reads but doesn't interpret). Data-loss prevention (compaction abort handling, concurrent safety) informs robustness assumptions about source SSTables.

<a id="tombstone-ttl"></a>
### Tombstones & TTL Purging  (16 files)

#### 🔴 High · `SSTableSkippingReadTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/distributed/org/apache/cassandra/distributed/test/SSTableSkippingReadTest.java`
- **Subsystem:** `org.apache.cassandra.io.sstable.format` · **~@Test methods:** 3
- **Purpose:** Validates that partition-level deletion tombstones correctly shadow data on other nodes and that skipped SSTables (due to min/max clustering bounds) are properly reincluded in reads when partition deletes may shadow remote data.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Partition delete recorded in minTimestamp of SSTable; SSTable skipped due to clustering bounds mismatch
  - Partition delete timestamp=1 shadows older data (timestamp=0) from remote node without resurrecting it
  - Skipped SSTable with partition delete is reincluded in merge when newer SSTable exists with different clustering range
  - Partition delete correctly shadows remote data even when SSTable was initially skipped from query planning
  - Multiple SSTable scenario: first sstable skipped, second sstable inserted with newer data; partition delete in first still shadows remote rows
  - Ensures partition deletion tombstones are not discarded during SSTable skipping optimization
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent data loss and incorrect reads: partition-level deletion tombstones must shadow older writes on other nodes, even when SSTables are skipped during query planning. Without proper handling, data deleted at the partition level could be resurrected from remote replicas, violating consistency guarantees and causing invisible data corruption.
- **CQLite relevance:** High relevance for CQLite compaction: when merging multiple SSTables (especially post-compaction), partition deletion tombstones recorded in minTimestamp must be preserved and correctly applied during row iteration. CQLite must account for partition deletes that shadow data in the merge output, and when reading skipped ranges with embedded partition tombstones, must not inadvertently drop those deletes. Format-level: partition deletes are encoded in row/cell tombstone structures and impact how CQLite interprets row visibility across SSTable boundaries.

#### 🔴 High · `RangeTombstoneBurnTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/distributed/org/apache/cassandra/fuzz/harry/examples/RangeTombstoneBurnTest.java`
- **Subsystem:** `org.apache.cassandra.fuzz.harry` · **~@Test methods:** 1
- **Purpose:** Property-based fuzzing of range tombstone correctness across memtable flushes and SSTable persistence. Validates that range deletes, row deletes, partition deletes, and column deletes serialize/deserialize correctly and produce correct query visibility without silent data loss.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Range delete correctness with clustering bounds and clustering key cardinality variation
  - Row deletion preservation across memtable-to-SSTable flush boundary
  - Partition-level tombstones correctly suppressing all rows in subsequent queries
  - Column-level deletions preserving undeleted columns across flush and SSTable storage
  - Range tombstone timestamp ordering vs. concurrent inserts (delete-then-insert vs. insert-then-delete)
  - Tombstone accumulation across multiple SSTable generations without compaction (to isolate merge semantics)
  - Query verification: selectRow and selectRowRange yield correct results matching model expectations after deletion operations
  - Fuzz-driven variation of deletion probabilities, partition sizes, and clustering key cardinality
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent data loss and incorrect query results by ensuring: (1) row/partition/column deletes are correctly serialized to SSTable Data.db and readable on deserialize; (2) range tombstones correctly suppress matching rows during iteration; (3) delete timestamp ordering is respected (deletes suppress older writes, not newer ones); (4) deletion markers persist across flush and multi-generation SSTable accumulation without corruption or suppression of non-deleted data.
- **CQLite relevance:** High. CQLite must correctly deserialize and apply range tombstone metadata from SSTable Data.db during row filtering: includes clustering-key range bounds, timestamps, deletion times. Range tombstones affect which rows are visible during iteration and compaction (merge logic must respect deletion boundaries and timestamp ordering). Without correct range tombstone handling, CQLite would return deleted rows or suppress undeleted ones during queries and compaction operations.

  - Range deletions spanning multiple index blocks are correctly merged and applied

- **CQLite relevance:** Critical for CQLite's compaction and read path. CQLite must correctly parse range tombstone markers from Data.db, respect their semantics when merging SSTables, and handle the case where tombstone boundaries appear in Index.db block summaries. Failure leads to incorrect filtering during merge compaction.

#### 🔴 High · `ViewComplexTombstoneTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/unit/org/apache/cassandra/cql3/ViewComplexTombstoneTest.java`
- **Subsystem:** `org.apache.cassandra.cql3` · **~@Test methods:** 2
- **Purpose:** Validates that cell tombstones and shadowable tombstones are correctly preserved during SSTable compaction when shadowed by newer row versions, preventing resurrection of deleted/null data in materialized views.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Cell tombstone shadowed by newer row is preserved during selective 2-SSTable compaction
  - Materialized view correctly filters rows when cell tombstones represent NULL column values
  - Tombstone behavior consistent across flush (on-disk SSTable) vs. no-flush (in-memory memtable) scenarios
  - Multi-version timestamp conflict resolution: later writes (TS=4) correctly shadow earlier tombstones (TS=2)
  - Selective compaction of middle SSTables does not affect newer tombstones that must be retained
  - WRITETIME() metadata preserved through tombstone shadowing and compaction
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent data resurrection where cell tombstones (used to represent NULL/deleted columns in views) are incorrectly purged during compaction, causing stale values to reappear in subsequent reads despite explicit NULL writes.
- **CQLite relevance:** High. CQLite's compactor must implement identical tombstone shadowing semantics during merge: cell-tombstones that are shadowed by newer row writes must persist in the output SSTable, not be elided. This is critical for correct read semantics—premature tombstone removal at compaction time causes data correctness bugs, not just write-path issues. CQLite readers must also handle this: merged SSTables with retained shadowed tombstones must be read back correctly.

#### 🔴 High · `TombstonesTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/unit/org/apache/cassandra/cql3/validation/miscellaneous/TombstonesTest.java`
- **Subsystem:** `org.apache.cassandra.cql3.validation.miscellaneous` · **~@Test methods:** 6
- **Purpose:** Validates that tombstone-overwhelming protection works correctly: exceptions are thrown when tombstone count exceeds thresholds, but not when tombstones are shadowed by partition deletes, expired past gc_grace_seconds, or below thresholds. Ensures query result safety against excessive tombstone scan overhead.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Queries with tombstone count below failure threshold complete without exception
  - Queries exceeding failure threshold raise TombstoneOverwhelmingException and increment failure metrics
  - Queries with tombstones shadowed by partition-level DELETE are not counted toward threshold
  - Live cells shadowed by partition DELETE do not trigger exceptions regardless of tombstone count
  - Tombstones beyond gc_grace_seconds are not counted; queries complete after gc_grace expiry even with high tombstone count
  - Queries with tombstone count between warn and failure thresholds increment warning metrics but succeed
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent data loss by blocking queries that would read an overwhelming number of tombstones and risk delivering incomplete results or timing out. Tombstone shadowing ensures that tombstones rendered moot by higher-precedence deletes are correctly excluded from the count, avoiding false-positive exceptions. gc_grace expiration ensures compaction-eligible tombstones are not counted, maintaining correctness of threshold logic.
- **CQLite relevance:** CQLite must implement equivalent tombstone-filtering logic during SSTable reading and compaction: (1) Filter cell-level tombstones shadowed by row/partition deletions to avoid inflating counts; (2) Exclude expired tombstones (based on local_deletion_time vs gc_grace_seconds) from result sets and compaction output; (3) Correctly distinguish between live cells and tombstones during merge to preserve result semantics. Failure here corrupts read results and compaction semantics.

- **Purpose:** Tests RangeTombstoneList merge semantics and search correctness for range deletion markers during compaction and read merging. Validates that overlapping tombstones are properly merged by timestamp precedence and that search queries correctly locate deletion times.

  - Overlapping ranges merged by taking higher timestamp (newer deletion wins)
  - Binary search on RangeTombstoneList finds deletion time for any clustering key value

If search is incorrect, cells could be returned despite deletion. Regression test #9799 guards against merge logic corrupting tombstone list state.
- **CQLite relevance:** HIGH - CQLite reads Cassandra 5.0 SSTables and must respect range tombstones during compaction merging. RangeTombstoneList merge semantics directly inform: (1) how CQLite merges tombstones from overlapping SSTables during compaction (identical merge-by-timestamp logic), (2) how CQLite filters cells at read time using searchDeletionTime() semantics, (3) how open-ended ranges (unbounded at partition/clustering bounds) are resolved during SSTable iteration and merge.

#### 🔴 High · `RangeTombstoneTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/unit/org/apache/cassandra/db/RangeTombstoneTest.java`
- **Subsystem:** `org.apache.cassandra.db` · **~@Test methods:** 14
- **Purpose:** Validates range tombstone semantics across memtable/SSTable boundaries, compaction merging, GC grace periods, and secondary index interactions. Tests ensure tombstones correctly kill rows, serialize with proper timestamps, and don't corrupt data during multi-SSTable compactions.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Row liveness correctly determined by overlapping range tombstones with different timestamps
  - Range tombstone slice filtering returns only applicable markers based on clustering key bounds
  - Timestamp metadata (min/max/localDeletionTime) tracked correctly through flush and compaction
  - GC grace period enforcement purges tombstones older than threshold during compaction
  - Overlapping range tombstones from multiple SSTables properly merged by timestamp winner
  - Range tombstone markers serialized correctly on disk after compaction (open/close pair structure)
  - Reverse clustering key iteration respects tombstone kills
  - Secondary index deletions triggered by range tombstone coverage
  - Column overwrites following range tombstone deletion properly update index state
  - Partition-level deletes interact correctly with range tombstones during merge
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent data loss when: (1) range tombstones lost during compaction merge, leaving supposedly-dead rows readable; (2) timestamp metadata corrupted, causing premature/delayed GC of tombstones; (3) overlapping tombstones from multiple SSTables not merged by timestamp, causing wrong rows to appear live; (4) secondary index state diverges from table due to incomplete tombstone processing. Detects on-disk structure corruption when compaction produces malformed marker pairs.
- **CQLite relevance:** Critical for compaction and merge correctness. CQLite compactor must: (1) parse range tombstone markers from Data.db and understand their [start, end] bounds in clustering space; (2) merge markers from multiple SSTables respecting timestamp ordering; (3) preserve marker pair structure (open + close) on output; (4) not lose tombstones during filtering for slice queries. Readers must correctly determine row liveness by checking if clustering key falls within any active tombstone range at query time.

#### 🔴 High · `RepairedDataTombstonesTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/unit/org/apache/cassandra/db/RepairedDataTombstonesTest.java`
- **Subsystem:** `org.apache.cassandra.db` · **~@Test methods:** 7
- **Purpose:** Validates that SSTable compaction correctly handles expired tombstone purging based on repair status — ensuring repaired tombstones are purged when gc_grace_seconds permits, while unrepaired tombstones are retained to prevent resurrections across replicas.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Compaction with repaired/unrepaired SSTable pairs: repaired tombstones are purged when both SSTable partition_keys indicate gc_grace expiry, unrepaired tombstones persist
  - Full SSTable drop: repaired SSTables containing only expired tombstones are dropped during compaction, while unrepaired counterparts are preserved
  - Read filtering: queries correctly filter expired tombstones from repaired SSTables using UnfilteredPartitionIterator.queryStorage() (internal visibility) vs executeLocally() (user-visible)
  - Row-level tombstones (DELETE ... WHERE partition_key AND clustering_key): purged from repaired SSTables, retained in unrepaired
  - Partition-level tombstones (DELETE ... WHERE partition_key only): purged from repaired SSTables, retained in unrepaired
  - Mixed repair timeline: oldest unrepaired SSTable in a sequence prevents all newer repaired tombstones from purging — tombstone retention is monotonic across repair status
  - Unrepaired-only case: all tombstones retained indefinitely when no SSTable has been marked repaired
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent data resurrection (read-after-delete violations) across repaired and unrepaired replicas: if repaired tombstones are incorrectly purged before unrepaired replicas can see them, a DELETE followed by a hint/gossip read can incorrectly return deleted data. Conversely, premature tombstone purging from unrepaired SSTables before repair consensus is reached can cause inconsistency. The test ensures SSTable metadata (repair_time in Statistics.db) gates purge eligibility.
- **CQLite relevance:** CQLite readers must parse and respect Statistics.db repair_time metadata when merging SSTables during compaction or export. The repair_time field determines whether an SSTable's tombstones are purgeable — CQLite compactors must not purge tombstones from unrepaired SSTables (repair_time == null) even if gc_grace_seconds has expired. During reads, purgeable tombstones may be filtered before merge/output, so CQLite's merge logic for compaction must check SSTableReader.isRepaired() / repair_time to gate purge eligibility correctly.

- **Scenarios:**
  - Concurrent multi-threaded mutation writes with fixed cell sizes across 4x CPU cores
  - Random-sized mutation payloads to exercise variable-length encoding paths
  - Mid-run segment discard and replay to verify post-break data not deleted and replayer rejects earlier mutations

  - Encryption-at-rest with commitlog read/write parity

- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Guards against silent data loss via hash-based round-trip verification: writes N mutations with computed hash, shuts down commitlog, replays all segments, re-computes hash from deserialized cells, and asserts equivalence. Detects: (1) cells lost in compression/encryption, (2) mutations skipped during replay, (3) segment boundaries corrupted, (4) deserialization errors masked by handlers. Critical for validating that commitlog recovery restores exact replica state post-crash.

#### 🔴 High · `CommitLogReaderTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/unit/org/apache/cassandra/db/commitlog/CommitLogReaderTest.java`

- **Purpose:** Validates commitlog segment reading, replay position recovery, mutation ordering, and corruption detection via sync marker checksum validation. Ensures commitlog segments can be correctly replayed during node restart and that corrupted segments are handled according to policy.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Reads all mutations from one or more commit log segments in sequence and verifies correct count
  - Reads a bounded count of mutations with early termination during replay
  - Resumes reading from a midpoint CommitLogPosition, skipping earlier segments by ID
  - Attempts to read more mutations than available from a midpoint, stopping at segment boundary
  - Detects and rejects corrupted sync markers (offset and CRC mismatch) when replay-error recovery is disabled
  - Ignores corrupted sync markers when COMMITLOG_IGNORE_REPLAY_ERRORS property is enabled
  - Silently skips corrupted segments when commit.failure.policy is set to ignore
  - Preserves row mutation order across replay, validating each row's data value matches insertion order
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents data loss during node restart by validating commitlog replay can correctly recover mutations from on-disk segments. Corruption detection prevents silent data loss from corrupted commitlog files: a failed sync marker checksum should either stop the node cleanly or be explicitly ignored per policy, rather than applying corrupted mutations. Position-based replay ensures idempotency by allowing resumption from a saved position without re-applying earlier mutations or missing any in the sequence.
- **CQLite relevance:** Low. CommitLog is write-ahead logging for in-flight durability; CQLite reads frozen SSTables (Data.db/Index.db/Statistics.db) that have already been flushed from memtable. However, understanding commitlog replay semantics informs SSTable generation: mutations flow commitlog → memtable → SSTable. The corruption detection pattern (checksum validation on write boundaries) mirrors SSTable checksums, which CQLite must validate during reads.

  - Ensures flushed buffer includes all data from start to end without padding beyond one fs-block boundary
  - Validates cumulative flush sizes match expected segment size via addSize() tracking
  - Confirms DirectIOSegmentBuilder creates fs-block-aligned direct memory buffers for segment I/O
  - Checks lastWritten offset is correctly updated after each flush operation
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents data loss from unaligned or incomplete commitlog writes. Misaligned direct-I/O writes risk kernel errors and partial durability; incorrect size tracking can lead to premature segment recycling while mutations remain unflushed to SSTable. Block alignment failures would corrupt on-disk commitlog format.
- **CQLite relevance:** N/A — commitlog is a write-path persistence mechanism (recovery log for in-flight mutations). CQLite is a read-only SSTable library; it never consumes commitlog files. The test exercises commitlog durability, not SSTable format parsing or compaction.

### Memtable & Flush  (10 files)

#### 🔴 High · `MemtableQuickTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/unit/org/apache/cassandra/db/memtable/MemtableQuickTest.java`
- **Subsystem:** `org.apache.cassandra.db.memtable` · **~@Test methods:** 4
- **Purpose:** Validates that memtable-to-SSTable flush preserves row and partition counts, tombstone semantics, and deleted-data filtering across four memtable implementations. Guards against silent data loss during flush-induced SSTable generation.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Writes 45K partitions with 4 rows each, verifies in-memory row count before flush
  - Deletes entire partitions (10K) via partition-level tombstones; verifies they return empty post-flush
  - Deletes individual rows (5K) via cell-level tombstones; verifies partial row visibility survives flush
  - Flushes memtable to SSTable and re-queries; asserts final row counts match expected (partitions minus deleted partitions minus deleted rows)
  - Validates estimated key count from SSTable cardinality estimator is within 10% of metadata row count
  - Tests all four memtable implementations (skiplist, skiplist_sharded, skiplist_sharded_locking, trie) for consistency
  - Verifies persistent memtables (streaming path) also produce correct SSTable statistics
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent loss of living cells during flush: detects if tombstones incorrectly purge future cells, if partition tombstones erase non-deleted rows, if row counts diverge between memtable and flushed SSTable, or if cardinality estimation drifts. Catches CASSANDRA-18123 (SSTable splitting under unified compaction incorrectly shrinking partition counts).
- **CQLite relevance:** High. CQLite must correctly (1) deserialize tombstone markers (partition-level and cell-level) in Data.db to produce same final row counts, (2) handle row-level deletion semantics during reads and merges, (3) preserve estimated key counts from Statistics.db for cardinality. If CQLite mis-interprets tombstone flags, loses count metadata, or fails to filter tombstoned rows during compaction merge, output will silently diverge from Cassandra ground truth.

While not direct data loss, failure causes unrecoverable memtable discard, losing all in-flight mutations. Guards against repair-induced churn (re-streaming with out-of-order timestamps) that triggers the bug in production.

If memtable accounting corruption causes silent cell retention or premature purging during flush-to-disk, CQLite readers see corrupt data. Medium-high relevance: CQLite doesn't write memtables but must correctly parse SSTable output from potentially buggy Cassandra. Monitors whether shadowed cells are correctly omitted from serialization and whether deletion markers propagate to disk reliably.

If CQLite implements compaction/merging, streamed SSTables (from repair/bootstrap) would arrive in this wire format before being persisted. Understanding this round-trip format ensures readers handle SerializationHeader variants correctly.

- **CQLite relevance:** Medium relevance. The cross-keyspace remapping scenario is less relevant (CQLite handles raw reads, not schema-driven remapping), but the data integrity and incomplete-file-handling scenarios inform compaction/merge correctness for readers that encounter partially-written or streamed files.

#### 🔴 High · `RepairedDataInfoTest.java`

- **Purpose:** Tests the RepairedDataInfo digest computation system used during anti-entropy repair to track and verify data consistency across replicas. Validates correct digest calculation including static rows, range tombstones, and garbage-collected data.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Counter correctly tracks row and partition limits during filtered iteration
  - Digest matches manually-computed hash for single partition with single row and empty static row
  - Digest matches manually-computed hash for single partition with multiple rows
  - Range tombstone markers are correctly included in digest calculation
  - Digest computation correctly handles multiple partitions with non-empty static rows
  - Fully purged partitions older than gc_grace_seconds produce empty digests
  - Empty partitions with no rows produce empty digests
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent divergence during repair by ensuring digest mismatches are detected when partitions contain incorrect or missing data. Incorrect digest computation could mask data inconsistencies between replicas, allowing silent data loss or corruption to persist undetected across the cluster.
- **CQLite relevance:** For CQLite as a reader: repair digests are metadata not stored in SSTables, so no direct format relevance. However, understanding how Cassandra computes digests over row data matters if implementing verification. For CQLite as a compactor: merging SSTables during compaction must preserve the logical row/tombstone structure that repair digests depend on. Incorrect merging could alter digest inputs. The tombstone and static row handling tested here are relevant to merge correctness.

#### 🔴 High · `PendingAntiCompactionTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/unit/org/apache/cassandra/db/repair/PendingAntiCompactionTest.java`
- **Subsystem:** `org.apache.cassandra.db.repair` · **~@Test methods:** 18
- **Purpose:** Validates pending anticompaction orchestration during incremental repair: SSTable acquisition by token range, metadata filtering (repair state, finalized sessions), transaction lifecycle, concurrent anticompaction blocking, and SSTable splitting to preserve data correctness during repair workflows.
- **Scenarios:**
  - SSTable acquisition by token range and filtering by repair metadata (repaired/unrepaired/pending)
  - Anticompaction splits multi-partition SSTables into repair-range-aligned subsets
  - Repair session conflict detection prevents simultaneous anticompactions on same SSTables
  - Transaction state transitions (IN_PROGRESS → ABORTED/COMMITTED) on acquisition success/failure
  - Concurrent anticompaction blocking via SSTableAcquisitionException and retry logic
  - Finalized/repaired SSTable exclusion via descriptor metadata mutation and reload
  - Secondary index SSTables remain untouched during main table anticompaction
  - Acquisition retries on transient failures with exponential backoff until timeout
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent data loss and incorrect repair state: ensures only unrepaired SSTables are marked pending, blocks conflicting anticompactions (avoiding duplicate repair or data skipping), validates SSTable splitting preserves all rows within token ranges, and enforces transaction abort on acquisition failure (preventing orphaned/inconsistent metadata).
- **CQLite relevance:** Medium — relevant for compaction merging. CQLite's compaction reader must correctly handle pending repair metadata (descriptor flags, session IDs) when merging SSTables and must respect token range boundaries during splits. Understanding concurrent acquisition blocking informs correctness of merge scheduling in write-support path (M5.2). However, anticompaction orchestration itself is Cassandra-specific; CQLite reads the resulting SSTables post-repair.

  - Handles transient (read-only) replicas: no data streamed toward them, asymmetric sync semantics
  - Generates optimized sync plans minimizing redundant streams (each range sourced from one node)
  - Coordinates local vs remote coordinators with pull-repair modes (initiator receives vs sends)
  - Purges empty differences: no sync tasks created when all replicas agree on all ranges
  - Handles multi-node clusters (5-node) with mixed transient replicas and complex consensus scenarios
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent divergence across replicas by ensuring merkle tree differences trigger synchronization. Memory leak in MerkleTrees (CASSANDRA-14096) could mask data inconsistencies indefinitely if SyncTask references weren't released. Transient replica handling prevents accidental data loss by disallowing streams toward read-only replicas, which would corrupt their state.
- **CQLite relevance:** Moderate. CQLite reads Cassandra 5.0 SSTables and may support compaction merging repaired SSTables. This test validates merkle tree difference detection (range hashing, per-range consistency) and sync coordination (which node pairs sync which ranges). For a compactor, understanding merkle semantics matters because repaired data is already consistent; compaction must preserve this consistency. The test ensures sync tasks don't create spurious ranges or corrupt transient replicas during merge."

#### 🟡 Med · `PaxosRepair2Test.java`

  - Prevents re-proposing stale ballots after repair by loading repair history from disk across node restarts
  - Forced repair does NOT update low-bound to allow legitimate re-proposals when quorum unavailable
  - Auto-repair processes uncommitted Paxos state when inflight proposals timeout
  - Purges committed Paxos state after grace period expires (TTL-based deletion)
  - Legacy TTL-based purge with grace periods correctly expires ballots older than gc_grace plus ballot age
  - Gracefully handles schema changes (keyspace drop) during in-flight repair coordination
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent acceptance of stale/conflicting proposals during leadership elections (Paxos phase 2); guards against corrupted consensus state being replayed after node recovery; ensures tombstone-like TTL semantics for expired proposals prevent resurrection of data committed in earlier rounds.
- **CQLite relevance:** N/A - CQLite reads user data SSTables (Data.db/Index.db), not the system Paxos table. Paxos repair affects consensus metadata stored in SSTables via flush/compaction, but CQLite does not implement repair or consensus logic. If CQLite were to export Paxos-affected tables, this test validates the durability guarantees that would apply to merged compacted data.

  - Accepts repair on compatible versions (4.1+)

Violation would cause inconsistent distributed consensus state and duplicate writes across replicas.

#### 🟡 Med · `PreviewRepairCoordinatorFastTest.java`

- **Purpose:** Validates repair preview mode coordination logic, error handling, and SSTable lifecycle safety. Tests both preview and full repair paths to catch data loss from incomplete/failed repair operations.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Repair succeeds on simple tables with single INSERT, generates correct JMX notifications and repair history
  - Repair rejects and exits cleanly on nonexistent keyspace/table without leaving orphaned state
  - Repair skips empty keyspace (no-op after filtering secondary index tables)

  - Repair succeeds as no-op for RF=1 clusters (no repair needed, no snapshot)
  - Repair fails gracefully when PREPARE_MSG protocol fails, tracks failure in repair_history for retry
  - Repair handles SNAPSHOT_MSG failures without leaking SSTables (verifies orphan cleanup)
  - Preview repair does not persist repair history (assertParentRepairNotExist), full repair does persist
  - Parameterized across sequential/parallel parallelism and with/without JMX notifications
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents data loss by: (1) detecting and cleaning up orphaned SSTables from failed snapshots via assertNoSSTableLeak(); (2) ensuring failed repairs are recorded in repair_history so retries can complete; (3) preventing partial repairs from appearing successful via assertParentRepairSuccess/Failed tracking; (4) verifying preview mode doesn't mutate on-disk files when coordination fails; (5) validating that invalid repair parameters fail fast without leaving cluster in inconsistent state.
- **CQLite relevance:** Medium relevance for SSTable reader/compactor. CQLite must read SSTables produced by completed repairs and handle merging/compacting across repair boundaries. The test's SSTable leak detection ensures files are in consistent state. Repair history tracking validates that repair metadata enables safe recovery after failures. When CQLite compacts SSTables from different repair generations, it must respect repair metadata to avoid silent data loss from orphaned/incomplete repairs.

CQLite reads heterogeneous SSTable sets across nodes and must correctly respect repair boundaries when merging data or producing compacted output. #### 🟡 Med · `AutoRepairSchedulerStatsVNodesTest.java`

Ensures accurate byte accounting across multiple keyspaces and tables during full repair cycles.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Flushes memtables to SSTables across 2 keyspaces with 2 tables each (100 rows per table) with compression disabled
  - Calculates expected repair bytes by summing onDiskLength() from CANONICAL SSTableReaders across all tables
  - Verifies totalBytesToRepair equals bytesAlreadyRepaired after full repair completion
  - Confirms totalKeyspaceRepairPlansToRepair (2) equals keyspaceRepairPlansAlreadyRepaired for both keyspaces
  - Validates repair start/finish timestamps and repair_turn reason in system_distributed.auto_repair_history
  - Ensures repair task duration is at least equal to SLEEP_IF_REPAIR_FINISHES_QUICKLY (2 seconds)

Conversely, overcounting could trigger unnecessary repairs. This test guards against bugs in SSTable metadata aggregation (onDiskLength summation) that would cascade into repair planning errors.
- **CQLite relevance:** Moderate. CQLite readers must correctly parse SSTable metadata headers and compute file-level statistics (onDiskLength, row/partition counts). The repair system uses these statistics to decide which SSTables need repair; if CQLite compaction/merging produces incorrect onDiskLength or omits valid data during file formatting, downstream repair metrics will be wrong. Also relevant for compaction drivers that consume merged SSTable stats to validate output files.

- **Scenarios:**
  - Completes both FULL and INCREMENTAL repair cycles within expected time window
  - Observes replica contention for INCREMENTAL repairs when parallel_replica_repair is disabled
  - Prevents cross-schedule contention when only per-replica parallelism is allowed
  - Records repair history with correct timestamps and repair turn reason (MY_TURN) in system.distributed
  - Collects and validates AutoRepairMetrics (nodeRepairTimeInSec, repairDelayedByReplica, repairDelayedBySchedule)
  - Allows parallel replica repair for FULL repairs without observed contention
  - Respects min_repair_interval and repair_check_interval configuration
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents inconsistency from unrepaired divergences by ensuring AutoRepair scheduler triggers and completes repair cycles, maintaining replica consistency. Validates that repair history is accurately recorded so operators can track when data was last reconciled across replicas.
- **CQLite relevance:** N/A — CQLite is a read-only SSTable parser. This test exercises repair coordination and scheduling logic (cluster-level, not on-disk format). However, when CQLite reads SSTables, it implicitly depends on repair having maintained replica consistency. This test ensures the repair scheduler itself works correctly, but CQLite does not invoke repair or parse repair history; CQLite only reads the resulting data files after repair completes.

#### 🟡 Med · `ForceRepairTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/distributed/org/apache/cassandra/distributed/test/repair/ForceRepairTest.java`
- **Subsystem:** `org.apache.cassandra.distributed.test.repair` · **~@Test methods:** 2
- **Purpose:** Validates that forced repair correctly handles node failures and repair type variations (full, incremental, preview, validation), and ensures repairedAt metadata is not incorrectly set when nodes are unavailable.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Repair fails gracefully when downstream nodes are offline
  - Forced repair succeeds despite initial repair failure and topology unavailability
  - Full repair with preview/validation modes execute without streaming to offline nodes
  - Incremental repair with preview/validation modes skip streaming when no differences
  - repairedAt timestamp is 0 (not set) after force repair when consistency verification was bypassed
  - Data consistency preserved when new mutations inserted during repair attempts
  - All repair types (full, incremental, with/without preview) tested against same failure scenario
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents incorrect repairedAt metadata assignment when repair is incomplete or forced past failures. Silent data loss occurs if downstream nodes incorrectly skip read-repair because they believe unverified data is already repaired. This test guards against that by asserting repairedAt=0 after forced repair.
- **CQLite relevance:** CQLite compactors must correctly read and preserve repairedAt from Statistics.db during merges. If a compactor misinterprets repairedAt=0 as "already repaired" (inverted logic) or loses the metadata during merge, read-repair coordination upstream could fail silently. This test validates the invariant that forced repair explicitly does NOT set repairedAt, which CQLite merges must respect.

#### 🟡 Med · `NoTombstonePurgingTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/distributed/org/apache/cassandra/distributed/test/repair/NoTombstonePurgingTest.java`
- **Subsystem:** `org.apache.cassandra.distributed.test.repair` · **~@Test methods:** 1
- **Purpose:** Validates that the --include-gcgs-expired-tombstones repair flag correctly streams expired tombstones to replicas during full repair, ensuring consistency before purging occurs.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Deletes row and waits for gc_grace_seconds to expire, making tombstone purgeable
  - Incremental repair does not stream expired tombstones to replicas (replica SSTables remain empty)
  - Full repair without -np flag does not stream expired tombstones (replica SSTables remain empty)
  - Full repair with --include-gcgs-expired-tombstones flag streams expired tombstones to replicas (replica SSTables become populated)
  - Validates that purgeable tombstones are handled differently during repair coordination depending on flag
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent data inconsistency: without -np, expired tombstones are purged locally without being streamed to replicas, potentially leaving stale data on other nodes if they were offline during gc_grace expiry. The test ensures -np causes expired tombstones to be streamed before purging, maintaining consistency across the cluster before final deletion.
- **CQLite relevance:** Medium relevance. CQLite reads SSTable Data.db containing tombstones and must (1) correctly deserialize deletion markers; (2) understand gc_grace_seconds semantics to distinguish purgeable from visible tombstones; (3) during compaction, correctly purge tombstones exceeding gc_grace without removing tombstones still within the grace period. This test validates the repair/streaming logic that determines which tombstones are safe to purge, informing compaction strategy in CQLite's own merge operations.

#### 🟡 Med · `RepairMetadataKeyspaceTest.java`

  - Tests repair streaming: repair mechanism reconstructs SSTables from data on nodes with complete files

For CQLite's compaction/merge logic, this is relevant because: (1) it ensures source SSTables feeding into compaction are complete and consistent across nodes, (2) it demonstrates that Cassandra's repair guarantees atomic SSTable restoration, which CQLite's compaction must preserve when re-writing merged SSTables, (3) the streaming and reconstruction path shown here informs what merge/compaction output must support when replaying after node failure.

  - Merges repair assignments with same token range and keyspace
  - Rejects merges with different token ranges or keyspaces via IllegalStateException
  - Splits repair assignments by BYTES_PER_ASSIGNMENT threshold and filters by MAX_BYTES_PER_SCHEDULE
  - Validates parameters (rejects unknown, enforces BYTES_PER_ASSIGNMENT <= MAX_BYTES_PER_SCHEDULE)
  - Plans keyspace-wide repairs with both per-table and per-range partition strategies
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent data loss by ensuring corrupted partitions are not skipped due to incorrect token range partitioning. Prevents repair jobs from exceeding byte budgets (which could cause crashes or incomplete repairs). Verifies SSTable size accounting so repair planners allocate correct resources.
- **CQLite relevance:** CQLite's compaction engine relies on accurate partition counts and token range boundaries to merge SSTables correctly. This test validates that on-disk size and partition estimation from SSTableReader is consistent across BTI/Big formats — essential for CQLite's merger to make sound scheduling decisions and avoid data loss during compaction.

#### 🟡 Med · `SSTableRepairedAtTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/unit/org/apache/cassandra/repair/autorepair/SSTableRepairedAtTest.java`
- **Subsystem:** `org.apache.cassandra.repair.autorepair` · **~@Test methods:** 6
- **Purpose:** Validates AutoRepair's SSTable repaired-state mutation logic. Tests verify that repair coordination correctly marks SSTables as repaired or unrepaired, handles preview vs. mutation modes, and properly resolves keyspace/table references.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Lists all tables belonging to a keyspace via StorageService discovery
  - Errors gracefully when keyspace does not exist (AssertionError with meaningful message)
  - Errors gracefully when table does not exist in keyspace (RuntimeException with table name)
  - Preview mode reports what would be mutated without changing repaired state (isRepaired remains false)
  - Mutation mode marks all live SSTables in table as repaired (isRepaired becomes true)
  - Unrepaired mode reverts previously-repaired SSTables back to unrepaired state (isRepaired becomes false)
  - Multi-SSTable scenarios (multiple flushes) mutate all SSTables consistently across affected files
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents inconsistent repair state by ensuring the repaired flag is correctly persisted and mutated. If repair state is not accurately tracked, repair operations may be skipped on already-repaired data or applied redundantly to unrepaired partitions, risking read-repair and hinting decisions. Preview mode prevents accidental bulk mutations that could corrupt repair tracking.
- **CQLite relevance:** CQLite reads the repaired flag from Statistics.db during metadata parsing. Repair state must be correctly preserved during compaction and export operations. If CQLite ignores or misinterprets the repaired flag, it could incorrectly merge repaired+unrepaired SSTables during compaction, violating Cassandra's repair-aware compaction invariants. Also relevant for streaming/export: the repaired flag must survive serialization to maintain cluster consistency.

#### 🟡 Med · `BulkRepairStateTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/unit/org/apache/cassandra/repair/consistent/BulkRepairStateTest.java`
- **Subsystem:** `org.apache.cassandra.repair.consistent` · **~@Test methods:** 5
- **Purpose:** Validates repair state merging logic and repaired-at timestamp tracking for token ranges. Ensures that repair operations correctly aggregate overlapping and nested ranges with correct repairedAt timestamps for data durability guarantees.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Merges overlapping token ranges from sequential repair operations with correct layering
  - Correctly handles identical ranges with newer repair timestamps superseding older ones
  - Absorbs smaller repaired ranges into larger ones at the same level
  - Partitions larger ranges when smaller nested ranges have different repair timestamps
  - Computes minimum repaired-at timestamp for arbitrary token ranges (unrepaired gaps return 0)
  - Maintains coverage of token ranges across multiple repair levels and timestamps
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent data loss by ensuring repair state tracking correctly tracks which token ranges have been repaired and when. If repaired-at timestamps are incorrect or ranges merge incorrectly, repair operations may skip sections of data or assume data is repaired when it isn't, allowing corrupted/divergent replicas to persist undetected.
- **CQLite relevance:** Medium relevance for CQLite compaction/merge operations: CQLite's compactor must respect repaired-at markers when merging SSTables. These tests validate the metadata structures used to determine repair status of token ranges—critical for ensuring CQLite's compaction doesn't incorrectly promote unrepaired data or lose repair provenance during SSTable merges.

#### 🟡 Med · `PendingRepairStatTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/unit/org/apache/cassandra/repair/consistent/PendingRepairStatTest.java`
- **Subsystem:** `org.apache.cassandra.repair.consistent` · **~@Test methods:** 1
- **Purpose:** Validates that pending repair state tracking correctly counts SSTables across repair sessions and accurately reflects session lifecycle transitions (PREPARING to FAILED/FINALIZED), ensuring repair coordination metadata remains consistent as SSTables are marked with repair UUIDs and session states change.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Creates multiple SSTables (sstable1, sstable2, sstable3) and assigns them to pending repair sessions
  - Mutates SSTable repair metadata (repairedAt timestamp, pendingRepair UUID) via compaction strategy manager to associate SSTables with specific repair sessions
  - Verifies PendingStats aggregation reports correct SSTable counts per session state (pending, failed, finalized)
  - Transitions repair sessions through state lifecycle (PREPARING -> FAILED or FINALIZED) and confirms stats accurately reflect state changes
  - Removes SSTables from pending repair sets by clearing pendingRepair UUID and validates cleanup completeness
  - Exercises SSTableReader.mutateRepaired() API for metadata mutation during repair coordination
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent data loss during repair by ensuring accurate tracking of which SSTables have pending repairs. If repair stats are undercounted or overcounted, repair completion can be incorrectly signaled, causing unrepaired data to be marked as repaired (violating replication factor guarantees). Detects incomplete cleanup when sessions transition to terminal states.
- **CQLite relevance:** CQLite reads SSTable Statistics.db files which contain repairedAt timestamp and pendingRepair UUID in the SerializationHeader. This test validates the write-side API that sets those metadata fields. A CQLite reader must correctly deserialize these fields to accurately determine whether an SSTable is pending repair. Metadata corruption during compaction or flush could cause CQLite to misread repair status, leading to incorrect query results or compaction decisions.

#### 🟡 Med · `SerializationsTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/unit/org/apache/cassandra/service/SerializationsTest.java`

- **Purpose:** Validates serialization and deserialization of repair protocol messages (ValidationRequest, ValidationResponse with Merkle trees, SyncRequest, SyncResponse) to ensure repair coordination can be properly communicated across cluster nodes and across protocol versions.

  - Serializes and deserializes ValidationResponse with empty Merkle trees (successful empty validation)
  - Serializes and deserializes ValidationResponse with populated split Merkle trees (successful validation with differences)

  - Round-trips SyncRequest with repair job descriptor, initiator/source/destination addresses, token ranges, and asymmetry flag
  - Serializes and deserializes SyncResponse with success state and SessionSummary list (streaming metadata including table IDs, byte counts)
  - Serializes and deserializes failed SyncResponse (success=false, empty summaries)
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent data inconsistencies during repair by validating that Merkle tree exchange, validation results, and sync metadata can be reliably communicated between nodes without corruption or loss of repair state. Incorrect deserialization would lead to repair failing silently or nodes disagreeing on validation/sync outcomes, leaving data inconsistencies unrepaired.
- **CQLite relevance:** Indirect. CQLite reads SSTable Data.db/Index.db but does not participate in repair. However, repair messages coordinate the detection and correction of data inconsistencies across replicas; CQLite readers benefit from the consistency that repair maintains. Merkle tree serialization is part of Cassandra's verification infrastructure—CQLite does not implement Merkle trees but could validate data integrity against checksums/repair results if integrated into a Cassandra cluster context.
