### Read Repair  (22 files)

#### 🔴 High · `ReadRepairCollectionQueriesTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/distributed/org/apache/cassandra/distributed/test/ReadRepairCollectionQueriesTest.java`
- **Subsystem:** `org.apache.cassandra.distributed.test.read.repair` · **~@Test methods:** 7
- **Purpose:** Validates read repair correctness for collection types (frozen/non-frozen tuples, sets, lists, maps, UDTs) under replica mismatch conditions. Tests ensure partial column selection and row/column deletions are correctly propagated during read-repair reconciliation at consistency level ALL.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Frozen tuple selection and repair: inserts tuple columns on one replica, queries subset with CL=ALL to verify partial repair propagation, then full row to verify complete repair
  - Non-frozen set element manipulation: inserts set, removes element on one replica, verifies element removal isn't propagated when not queried, then propagates on full-row query
  - Non-frozen list element deletion: inserts list, deletes indexed element on one replica, verifies list state reconciles correctly via read repair
  - Non-frozen map key deletion: inserts map, deletes map key on one replica, verifies key deletion propagates only when queried column overlaps
  - Frozen UDT field selection: inserts UDT, queries specific field with CL=ALL, verifies only selected field is repaired initially
  - Non-frozen UDT field deletion: inserts UDT, deletes field on one replica, verifies field deletion propagates correctly during read repair
  - Row deletion propagation: deletes entire row on one replica, verifies read-repair propagates row deletion to all replicas with CL=ALL
  - Parameterized matrix coverage: tests execute with 8 BLOCKING and 1 NONE repair strategy variants, 2 coordinator choices, memtable vs SSTable storage, and paging enabled/disabled
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Guards against silent data divergence in replicated collections. Prevents: (1) stale collection values returned when replicas differ, (2) inconsistent collection deletions where one replica purges elements/fields but others retain them, (3) loss of row deletion semantics across replicas if read-repair fails to propagate DELETE FROM queries. Tests verify that CL=ALL reads force convergence, preventing future reads from returning inconsistent partial collection state.
- **CQLite relevance:** N/A — read-repair is a cluster coordination feature, not an SSTable format or merge/compaction concern. CQLite reads immutable on-disk SSTables and does not implement read-repair protocol. However, understanding collection serialization correctness (frozen tuple, set, map, UDT encoding in Data.db) informs CQLite's collection deserialization logic and validates that collection values are correctly round-tripped through SSTable encoding/decoding. Test scenarios implicitly verify collection binary format stability across compaction/repair cycles.

#### 🔴 High · `SSTableAndMemTableDigestMatchTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/unit/org/apache/cassandra/db/SSTableAndMemTableDigestMatchTest.java`
- **Subsystem:** `org.apache.cassandra.db` · **~@Test methods:** 8
- **Purpose:** Validates that digest computation across memtable and SSTable reads produces consistent results after flush. Ensures memtable and disk data produce identical digests regardless of column filtering, protecting read-repair correctness.
- **Scenarios:**
  - All-columns select on flush matches pre-flush digest
  - Selective column filtering (empty, non-empty) maintains digest equivalence
  - Empty complex column cell selection computes same digest pre and post-flush
  - Non-empty complex column cell selection (maps) maintains consistency
  - Static-only partitions with null/non-null/deleted static columns digest correctly
  - Mixed static column updates (add, overwrite, delete) preserve digest across flush
  - Cell-level filtering for specific map keys matches before/after flush
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent digest mismatches during read-repair that could cause data inconsistency between replicas. A failing digest comparison means either SSTable serialization dropped cells or memtable digest computation is incorrect—either causes silent read-repair failures where inconsistent replicas appear consistent, leading to selective data loss on future reads.
- **CQLite relevance:** Medium. CQLite reads Data.db and merges compacted SSTables; digest computation is read-repair infrastructure, not core to CQLite's open/scan/export APIs. However, the test validates that SSTable serialization (on write) and deserialization (on read) are symmetric—critical for CQLite's compaction merge semantics. If cells are dropped during SSTable write, CQLite's compaction logic would silently lose data when re-merging generations.

#### 🟡 Med · `ReadRepairEmptyRangeTombstonesWithFlushesTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/distributed/org/apache/cassandra/distributed/test/ReadRepairEmptyRangeTombstonesWithFlushesTest.java`
- **Subsystem:** `org.apache.cassandra.distributed.test` · **~@Test methods:** 7
- **Purpose:** Validates that read repair correctly handles range tombstones across replicas without emitting spurious empty range tombstones (CASSANDRA-15924). The flush mode ensures tombstones are persisted to SSTable format, testing serialization correctness.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Empty range queries on a partition with range tombstones in one replica correctly return 0 rows and synchronize tombstones
  - Empty range queries with static rows preserve static data while applying range tombstone repairs
  - Range queries returning rows inside a tombstone range on one replica correctly surface live rows and sync tombstones
  - Range queries overlapping tombstone range boundaries correctly identify live rows outside bounds and propagate tombstones
  - Point queries retrieving rows contained within a tombstone range correctly return live data and repair divergent tombstones
  - Point queries with static rows correctly sync static data despite range tombstones on one replica
  - Memtable flush serializes range tombstones to SSTable Data.db format for accurate repair merge semantics
  - Reverse clustering order and paging preserve correct tombstone merge behavior across repair coordination
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent data loss when range tombstones diverge across replicas: without correct handling, deleted rows could reappear on reads, or live rows could be incorrectly marked deleted during repair. Ensures tombstone propagation is minimal (avoids empty range tombstones) to prevent future write blocking.
- **CQLite relevance:** HIGH: CQLite's SSTable reader and compactor must deserialize range tombstones from flushed Data.db files and correctly merge them across SSTable generations during compaction. This test validates that on-disk serialization of range tombstones is correct, which directly impacts CQLite's ability to read and compact Cassandra files accurately. The flush variant specifically tests SSTable format correctness.

Row deletion must propagate to avoid returning deleted rows on future queries.
- **CQLite relevance:** Low. CQLite reads SSTables sequentially and does not handle point queries or read repair coordination. However, understanding row and cell-level deletion semantics (tombstone markers, DELETE cell markers, row deletion markers) is relevant to correct SSTable merge and compaction behavior—CQLite must properly serialize and merge these deletion markers across multiple SSTable generations during compaction.

The test validates that partial deletes (column-level tombstones) and full row deletes repair correctly through the read-repair path.
- **CQLite relevance:** N/A for reader—CQLite reads SSTables at rest and does not implement read repair coordination. However, CQLite's compaction/merge logic must correctly handle tombstone markers and deletion timestamps that result from read-repair writes. #### 🟡 Med · `ReadRepairTest.java`

  - Partition deletion with timestamp ties does not generate unnecessary range tombstone bounds (CASSANDRA-16453)
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent data loss by validating that read repair correctly identifies and heals incomplete replicas. Critical for preventing stale reads when replica inconsistency occurs; also prevents unnecessary resource-intensive repairs triggered by tombstones that are already GC-eligible or purgeable.
- **CQLite relevance:** Low relevance to CQLite. CQLite reads SSTables with a unified view (no cluster coordination). However, the tombstone resurrection tests (CASSANDRA-11427, CASSANDRA-8989) inform correct merging semantics during compaction: tombstones within gc_grace_seconds must be preserved across SSTable merges, and range tombstone interactions with row tombstones must not cause spurious repairs. For a compactor/merger in CQLite, these behaviors validate that purged tombstones are not resurrected during multi-SSTable compaction merge.

### Secondary Index (SAI/SASI)  (10 files)

#### 🔴 High · `ColumnIndexTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/unit/org/apache/cassandra/io/sstable/format/ColumnIndexTest.java`
- **Subsystem:** `org.apache.cassandra.io.sstable.format` · **~@Test methods:** 2
- **Purpose:** Validates column index block boundary handling in SSTable formats (BIG and BTI) when range tombstone boundary markers appear at index block edges, preventing silent data loss during partition queries with paging.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Queries with clustering conditions and paging (page size=1) correctly read rows when RTBM is the last item in column index block
  - Both BIG and BTI formats handle RTBM at index block boundary identically
  - Range tombstone boundary markers do not cause skipped or lost rows in paginated result sets
  - Multiple mutations creating RTBMs followed by flush and query return correct row count (3 rows per partition)
  - Index block boundaries correctly account for tombstone markers without truncating live cells in subsequent blocks
  - Silent data loss is prevented when reading partitions with range deletions and inserts that generate boundary markers
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent data loss: RTBMs at index block boundaries could cause readers to skip live cells in subsequent blocks if the index boundary calculation incorrectly treats the tombstone marker as a partition end. This test confirms rows after such boundaries are not silently lost during paging.
- **CQLite relevance:** High relevance for CQLite as a reader: CQLite's Index.db and row index (BTI) parsing must correctly handle column index blocks where RTBMs appear at boundaries. Incorrect boundary handling would cause CQLite to skip cells or misalign reads across block boundaries. The merge/compaction logic must also preserve cells after tombstone boundaries to avoid data loss during compaction.

#### 🔴 High · `PartitionIndexTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/unit/org/apache/cassandra/io/sstable/format/bti/PartitionIndexTest.java`
- **Subsystem:** `org.apache.cassandra.io.sstable.format.bti` · **~@Test methods:** 14
- **Purpose:** Validates the BTI (Block-level Trie Index) partition index implementation, specifically testing exact and range lookups (equality, greater-than, less-than), bounded iteration, pointer encoding across file growth, and resilience to corruption.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Exact key lookup (exactCandidate) on random/sequential/long keys up to 245K entries
  - Greater-than/greater-or-equal ceiling queries with random query keys and boundary validation
  - Less-than floor queries with neighbor validation
  - Constrained range iteration (between left/right keys) across 1000 random ranges with exact/inexact boundaries
  - Empty byte key handling and single-entry indexes
  - Full and partial index iteration with multi-part flushing (15-part segmented builds)
  - Pointer growth validation across file boundaries (2^16 to 2^42 byte offsets)
  - Deep recursion protection for keys with long common prefixes (PageSize+99 character keys)
  - Corruption detection when garbage is injected into index file mid-write
  - Trie node type diversity (validates 7+ different node types generated across pointer sizes)
  - Index dumping to file and verification
  - Both mmap and standard disk access modes
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent data loss and incorrect reads by validating: (1) exact partition lookups never return wrong partition, (2) range queries correctly enforce boundary semantics, (3) corrupted index files raise exceptions rather than returning stale offsets, (4) pointer encoding handles 64-bit offsets correctly as file grows, (5) iteration does not skip or duplicate partitions during merge/streaming operations.
- **CQLite relevance:** Critical for CQLite compaction and streaming. The partition index is the primary on-disk lookup structure for locating partition boundaries in Data.db. CQLite's compactor must correctly read partition index boundaries to merge SSTables without row loss or duplication. Corruption or off-by-one errors in pointer encoding or range queries could cause silent data loss or incorrect merge results during compaction.

#### 🔴 High · `RowIndexTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/unit/org/apache/cassandra/io/sstable/format/bti/RowIndexTest.java`
- **Subsystem:** `io.sstable.format.bti` · **~@Test methods:** 7
- **Purpose:** Validates BTI (Bytes Trie Index) row index format correctness for SSTable partition lookup. Tests ensure round-trip serialization of clustering key indexes, correct ordering enforcement, and proper range iteration semantics for finding row partitions within Data.db files.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Separator-floor lookups return correct partition offsets for singleton keys (8192 random UUIDs)
  - Multi-row spans preserve clustering key ranges with embedded DeletionTime metadata
  - Out-of-order key addition is caught and rejected via AssertionError
  - Duplicate key additions throw AssertionError to prevent silent overwrites
  - Empty clustering keys (BOTTOM/TOP sentinels) correctly mark partition boundaries
  - Reverse iteration from right bound visits matching keys in descending order
  - Constrained range iteration respects left/right bounds for both exact and generated keys
  - DeletionTime (markedForDeleteAt/localDeletionTime) correctly round-trips in IndexInfo
  - Byte-comparable key ordering preserved through index serialization
  - Parameterized testing over disk access modes (mmap vs standard)
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent data loss from: (1) out-of-order key insertion corrupting index sort order and causing incorrect row selection, (2) duplicate keys overwriting partition metadata, (3) broken separator-floor lookups returning wrong partition offsets, (4) corrupted DeletionTime in index entries causing TTL/tombstone mishandling, (5) BOTTOM/TOP sentinel corruption during deserialization causing range scan boundaries to be wrong.
- **CQLite relevance:** HIGH - CQLite must deserialize BTI trie indexes (RowIndexReader) and execute separator-floor lookups to locate row partitions in Data.db. This test validates: (1) key ordering invariants that cqlite's binary search/trie traversal depends on, (2) range iteration correctness for full-table/range scans, (3) metadata encoding (DeletionTime) that affects row-level TTL decoding, (4) BOTTOM/TOP sentinel handling critical for partition boundary detection.

- **CQLite relevance:** CQLite reads index summaries (Summary.db and Index.db) for partition-key binary search; this test is highly relevant as it validates summaries remain correct after downsampling/upsampling. #### 🔴 High · `IndexSummaryTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/unit/org/apache/cassandra/io/sstable/indexsummary/IndexSummaryTest.java`

- **Purpose:** Validates IndexSummary construction, serialization, key lookup, and downsampling behavior—the sparse partition index that guides binary search during SSTable seeks.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Constructs IndexSummary with varying key sizes (32-10000 bytes) and entry counts, verifying metadata accuracy (minIndexInterval, maxNumberOfEntries, estimatedKeyCount)
  - Detects runtime buffer overflow when actual key sizes exceed predictions, truncating entries to partial coverage
  - Detects construction-time buffer overflow and auto-adjusts minIndexInterval to downsample while maintaining full SSTable coverage
  - Performs binary search lookups on summary to locate partition keys
  - Serializes and deserializes IndexSummary to byte format; validates round-trip fidelity with correct position/key recovery
  - Handles edge case of empty (zero-length) keys through serialization cycle
  - Progressively downsamples sparse index (128→64→32→...→1 entries) using deterministic pattern, preserving key order and scan positions
  - Validates position lookup (getScanPosition) correctness across downsampling levels
  - Verifies reverse-mapping of downsampled index entries to original full-resolution indices
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent partition location errors and incorrect reads during SSTable binary search. Wrong IndexSummary serialization, downsampling, or position calculation causes binary search to return incorrect offsets, leading to skipped partitions or reads from wrong data blocks—manifesting as missing rows from the reader's perspective.
- **CQLite relevance:** CQLite's SSTable reader deserializes IndexSummary to perform partition key binary search on Index.db, which guides efficient seeks within Data.db. Downsampling behavior is critical for readers with memory constraints. Serialization round-trip is mandatory for persisted Index.db reading.

- **CQLite relevance:** Moderately relevant. CQLite reads Data.db/Index.db directly and performs compaction/merging. This test validates that: (a) checksum validation catches corrupted SSTable metadata before merge operations ingest it (prevents silent corruption propagation through compaction), (b) SAI index file format integrity via validateChecksum—a pattern CQLite should mirror for BTI/other index formats during read/merge. The test does not directly exercise Data.db format parsing or row-level correctness (Cassandra's domain), but demonstrates checksum-first defense strategy applicable to CQLite's compaction and cross-generation format compatibility."

- **Scenarios:**

  - Virtual table is empty after memtable insert but before flush
  - Virtual table shows index metadata after single SSTable flush
  - Virtual table accumulates entries across multiple SSTable flushes
  - New index creation adds corresponding entries per existing SSTable
  - SSTable with sparse indexable columns (nulls) creates partial index entries
  - SSTable with mixed sparse columns creates multiple index entries (one per indexed column)

  - Table drop clears all virtual table entries for that table

However, it indirectly guards against silent data loss by verifying that SSTable index metadata (cell counts, row ID ranges, token bounds) is correctly tracked and updated across lifecycle events (flush, compaction, drop). If metadata becomes stale or inconsistent, read queries through SAI could skip SSTables or return incorrect row counts, leading to silent data loss or incorrect results.
- **CQLite relevance:** Low — CQLite is a read-only SSTable parser; it does not use Storage-Attached Index (SAI) metadata for data retrieval. The test validates metadata consistency (cell counts, row ID ranges, token bounds) that a reader or compactor might rely on for optimization or verification, but CQLite's current scope does not depend on SAI.

#### 🟡 Med · `MultiNodeTableWalkWithoutReadRepairTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/distributed/org/apache/cassandra/distributed/test/cql3/MultiNodeTableWalkWithoutReadRepairTest.java`
- **Subsystem:** `org.apache.cassandra.distributed.test.cql3` · **~@Test methods:** 1
- **Purpose:** Property-based stateful testing of multi-node CQL operations without read repair. Validates data consistency across randomized query patterns (selects, inserts, compaction, repair) on 3-node clusters with consistency level ALL.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Multi-node cluster initialization with consistency level ALL queries
  - Randomized INSERT operations on tables with various CQL types (primitives, collections, UDTs, vectors)
  - Full table scans and partition-restricted queries (by PK, token, token range)
  - Clustering column BETWEEN queries with range conditions
  - Memtable flush to SSTable triggering
  - Compaction of multiple SSTables
  - Incremental and preview repair operations
  - Index creation on arbitrary columns and SAI-indexed query execution
  - WITHOUT read repair (ReadRepairStrategy.NONE) validates behavior under unrepaired inconsistencies
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Detects silent data loss across multi-node clusters during compaction, flush, and repair when read repair is disabled. Validates that unrepaired data inconsistencies do not result in incorrect query results or corrupted SSTables. Ensures mutations appear consistently via ALL consistency level despite node-local write semantics.
- **CQLite relevance:** HIGH. CQLite reads merged SSTables produced by Cassandra compaction. This test validates compaction correctness (preserves all mutations, resolves conflicts by timestamp) across multi-node clusters, which is critical for a reader that must handle arbitrary SSTable merge states. The WITHOUT read repair variant specifically exercises unrepaired SSTables, which a compactor must handle correctly. Flush operations validate that SSTable serialization preserves data identity across memtable-to-SSTable transitions, a prerequisite for CQLite's merge logic.

#### 🟡 Med · `CorruptionTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/long/org/apache/cassandra/cql3/CorruptionTest.java`
- **Subsystem:** `org.apache.cassandra.cql3` · **~@Test methods:** 1
- **Purpose:** Stress-tests concurrent INSERT and SELECT operations on the same key with large blob values (500KB+) under high throughput to detect data corruption, silent drops, or read mismatch anomalies across 40 concurrent threads.
- **Scenarios:**
  - 40 concurrent threads each performing 100,000 write-read cycles on the same key with 500KB blob values
  - Detects silent data corruption where inserted value does not exactly match retrieved value (byte array mismatch)
  - Tests under heavy write/read contention to surface race conditions or buffer corruption in memtable/SSTable lifecycle
  - Timeouts on hot keys are acceptable (test verifies correctness when responses arrive)
  - Uses LOCAL_QUORUM consistency to exercise node-local coordination
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Detects silent data corruption or substitution where written blobs are retrieved with incorrect content — a critical failure mode where data appears intact (row exists) but its value is wrong, bypassing simple row-existence checks and exposing serialization/deserialization defects, memtable flushing bugs, or SSTable read-path corruption.
- **CQLite relevance:** Relevant for CQLite's SSTable reader as a validation that read-path byte-accurate reconstruction works: CQLite must deserialize blob columns identically to how Cassandra stored them. The test's focus on large (500KB) unstructured blob values under concurrent load mirrors conditions where Data.db decompression, block reads, or column value assembly could fail silently. CQLite's compaction path (if implemented) must preserve blob byte-for-byte fidelity when merging SSTables.

#### 🟡 Med · `FlushIndexWhileQueryingTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/unit/org/apache/cassandra/index/sai/plan/FlushIndexWhileQueryingTest.java`
- **Subsystem:** `org.apache.cassandra.index.sai` · **~@Test methods:** 1
- **Purpose:** Validates that memtable flush (SSTable creation) during concurrent index query execution does not cause data loss or inconsistent results. Ensures query correctness when flush transitions in-memory data to on-disk index structures during active searches.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Flush SSTable and secondary index while equality query is in-flight
  - Query returns correct row count (2 rows) despite concurrent memtable transition
  - IndexSearchResultIterator correctly handles mix of pre-flush and post-flush index data
  - Barrier synchronization prevents race conditions between query and flush phases
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents silent loss of query results (phantom reads) when SSTable flush races with secondary index query execution. A failure would mean queries miss rows present in the newly flushed index, leading to incomplete result sets.
- **CQLite relevance:** Low. CQLite is a read-only SSTable parser; it does not perform live flushes or handle concurrent query/write interleaving. However, the test validates that data written to disk during flush is complete and queryable—behavior that CQLite's parser must assume holds true. If flush could corrupt or partially write index data, CQLite's reads could fail unpredictably.

- **Scenarios:**
  - Measures GC pressure and performance during bulk INSERT of large partitions with multiple 8-KiB rows per partition
  - Validates flush operation performance across partition size spectrum (16 KiB to 8 GiB)
  - Measures compaction throughput and key cache efficiency when merging large partitions
  - Performs 50,000 random single-row point lookups with key cache hit rate tracking
  - Performs full-partition scans with iteration performance and memory cache metrics
  - Tracks key cache capacity, size, entries, hit rate, and 1-minute hit rate throughout lifecycle
  - Establishes baseline for GC impact of partition size variance in write/read paths
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Primarily performance and resource measurement; validates that large partitions can be written, flushed, compacted, and read without memory exhaustion or hang conditions that could lead to data loss through node failure or silent corruption during compaction merge.
- **CQLite relevance:** Relevant to CQLite compaction/merge for large partitions: validates that partition boundary detection, row iteration, and key cache behavior remain correct and performant at scale; CQLite's compactor must handle multi-gigabyte partitions without memory explosion or incorrect merge semantics.

#### 🟡 Med · `SSTablesGlobalTrackerTest.java`
- **Path:** `test/unit/org/apache/cassandra/service/SSTablesGlobalTrackerTest.java`
- **Subsystem:** `org.apache.cassandra.service` · **~@Test methods:** 1
- **Purpose:** Validates that SSTablesGlobalTracker correctly maintains the set of SSTable versions currently in use across sequential add/remove operations, preventing stale version information from corrupting compaction or streaming decisions.
- **Scenarios:**
  - Property-based testing generates 0-100 random SSTable add/remove events per test case
  - Verifies tracker computes correct set of versions in use after each update
  - Validates change-notification signal (triggerUpdate) fires only when version set changes
  - Tests across multiple SSTable format types (BTI, legacy, etc.)
  - Ensures tracker handles mixed adds/removes with empty removed or added lists
  - Validates idempotence: applying same version set multiple times yields same result
- **Data-loss / correctness guard:** Prevents compaction logic from operating on stale version metadata, which could cause SSTable files with incompatible formats to be incorrectly merged, or live files to be skipped during compaction rounds, resulting in silent data loss or inaccessible partitions.
- **CQLite relevance:** Low-medium. CQLite's compaction/merge implementation must respect version compatibility when selecting SSTables to merge, but CQLite does not track live SSTable lifecycles; it operates on pre-selected SSTable files. Understanding version compatibility matters for merge correctness but not for this tracker's orchestration role.

- **CQLite relevance:** N/A — this test is about Full Query Log serialization and replay semantics, not SSTable format/layout/parsing. It does not exercise Data.db/Index.db on-disk structures, compression, BTI indexes, or compaction logic that CQLite reads. The tombstone behavior validated here (NULL equals deletion) is application-level; CQLite only reads the already-persisted SSTable cells and tombstone markers, not the query log tool that produces them.
